### Subject: FOR INPUT: SPO 2016-2017 Integrated Corporate Plan (Step 1) Due Fri Sept 4

Hi Bruce – Apologies for the delay but here is the official call-out for SPO's input to the 2016-2017 Integrated Corporate

## Plan (ICP).

For this exercise, OMC will have a three-step approach:

Step 1:

### Reflection on the Planning Process

and Setting of the Branch Work Plan (Milestones)

Step 2: OMC Input to the OPS Sector Risk Profile (new for 2016-2017)

Step 3: Identification of Resource Requirements / Resource Enablers:

### a) Financial Systems Plan

b) IM/IT Plan
### c) HR Planning and Staffing Planning

d) Accommodations National Program of Work requirements
### e) Procurement Plan requirements

# f) Communications plan

At this point in time, we are completing Step 1, and are providing you with the following to guide and record your planning:

1. ICP Reflection Exercise.docx [http://gcdocs2/otcs/cs.exe/properties/60983179](http://gcdocs2/otcs/cs.exe/properties/60983179)
### 2. Instructions on how to complete the Milestone Tracking Template (attached to this email message)

### 3. Your Division's Milestone Tracking Template [http://gcdocs2/otcs/cs.exe/properties/61102563](http://gcdocs2/otcs/cs.exe/properties/61102563)

Timelines associated with the 2016-2017 planning process are very tight as the entire exercise has been moved up by two months at the request of the DM and ExCom.

The deadline for Step 1 is Friday, September 4th by which time you must have submitted both your completed “Reflection” exercise and your Milestone Tracking Template to the Program Coordination Unit (Jennifer Poulin, with cc to Lorraine Savage and Chantal Gagnon). From these two documents, the PCU will then compile OMC's Branch Work Plan (Milestones) for DG review and further discussion within OMC at a special Directors' Meeting being planned for

## Monday, September 14th.

## 2016-2017 ICP: Reflection Exercise

## Introduction

Prior to identifying the business and ongoing management initiatives that OMC will focus on achieving over the short- term (one year horizon), we must first assess our environment. Then, we must use this assessment to identify relevant requirements for the Sector Plan (i.e., Milestones, Corporate Risk elements, HR Milestones, Procurement Plan elements, etc.). This assessment is intended to support you in identifying the internal and external business conditions within which your Division and/or our Branch can expect to undertake our work next year which will impact-positively and negatively – our ability to deliver on results. It also will help you identify risks and opportunities which you can then use to develop your various work (milestone) and resource plan components.

## Reflexion Exercise

Step 1: Individual Reflection on the Planning Process (current document) Step 2: Branch Discussion Step 3: Articulation of the Branch discussion in writing

## Instructions

This current exercise is intended to guide your development of milestones for 2016-2017.

## Please complete the questions asked in this document by providing high-level or point form responses/details.

When you are ready to complete this document electronically, start your answers under the tables provided (or you can simply delete the tables); the tables / lines are provided for those who prefer to write this out by hand first. Both your completed Reflection and Milestone Master Template are due to the PCU by Friday, September 4th, for DG and Branch Review the following week. The results of the Branch Discussion will be noted and provided to OPMB (who coordinate the OPS Sector input) by the

# PCU as part of the overall 2016-2017 ICP planning.

## The Operational Management and Coordination (OMC) Branch is responsible for overseeing and managing the delivery

of immigration programs across the service delivery network, which includes;

●

# delivering functional guidance on all aspects of immigration programs, domestically and internationally, in a

## coherent and integrated network;

coordinating all immigration-related operations of the Department by: oimplementing new policies, legislation and regulations; o providing functional guidance; o leading key modernization projects; oleading Government initiatives for the Operations Sector (e.g., Refugee Reform, 2015 Pan American

# Games);

ostrengthening partnerships with Citizenship and Immigration Canada's (CIC) stakeholders internally and externally; o managing the content of application forms and guides (kits); managing the Web Computer Assisted Response tool (WebCART); o odeveloping, writing, revising, editing and publishing Program Delivery Instructions (PDIs), formerly

# known as Manuals and Operational Bulletins (OBs); and

managing the Operational Readiness mailbox.

OMC plays an integral role in shaping the Modernization agenda by: ensuring that linkages are made across the network between all aspects of CiC_immigration programs to achieve seamless delivery; supporting an integrated, global network to increasingly process applications where capacity exists, not necessarily where they are submitted; having tools that will bring efficiencies to the CIC network such as e-Applications for Temporary Residents; modernizing the delivery of functional guidance (i.e., PDIs) by ensuring information is provided in a consistent manner, up-to-date and easy to find;

# working continuously to simplify application kits and forms; and

managing CIC's workload with an effective and systematic risk management approach.

## Individual Reflexion Questions

Describe what, if anything, listed in the description of the Branch that needs to be changed or updated to more accurately reflect our current operating environment, responsibilities, etc. See tracked changes above.

Answer the questions asked below with regard to the following:

a) Milestones: •What is the work that carries over from 2015-2016? CContinue development and implementation of eTA Expansion. 0 Continue working with the CBSA to develop and implement the Entry-Exit project, and eventually use this information to bolster ClC immigration screening.
- What is possible new work for 2016-2017? Beyond the Border "2.0."
b) Branch Context and Contributions – Priorities:
- What are your Division's priorities for 2016-2017? CImplement eTA Expansion. CImplement predictive analytics into eTA screening. GWork with the CBSA to develop and implement Entry-Exit. c Leverage Entry-Exit information to bolster CIC immigration screening. What components of these priorities are part of our Branch's existing work and mandate, and which ones may add or change our work and mandate? NII.
c) Risks: Which Corporate Risks does the Branch mitigate? c Depending on partners to support delivery of programs and services. What are the branch risks? There is a risk that the CBSA will not have fully rolled out its Interactive Advance Passenger Information (IAPI) system on schedule (i.e., in March 2016). What is the probability of the risk occurring and what is the impact if it does occur? c Probability high, impact medium What is the branch doing to mitigate these risks? CWorking with the CBSA at all levels to provide necessary support to ensure testing with commercial airlines begins well in advance of March 15, 2016, with an aim to having "onboarded" a critical mass of commercial airlines by March 15, 2016.
d) Resource Requirements / Resource Enablers: What do we need to achieve our Branch Plan (Milestones)? What is our budget? Do ExCom decisions on the carry forward exercise affect our resources? How?
The eTA budget is fenced.

# ExCom decisions on funding are required for eTA Expansion.

CIC received a portion of the Entry-Exit budget. What are our divisional HR requirements? Do we have the right team in place? Are there gaps? Do we have the needed skill sets? Are there gaps? Are there shifts in skill sets? Nil.

# What are staff movement expectations (retirements, temporary absences, etc.)? Numbers

affected? How will this be addressed? 3

## Hi everyone,

As per the email chain below, back in November we'd put the PDI conversion of the R11 OP1 sections (5.21 and 5.23) on hold.

It remains in the regulation plan, but is not currently being worked on. As such, I've been asked to revive this Manual to PDI work in order to support the continued elimination of manual OP1.

# The proposed updates to the PDI [http://cicintranet.ci.gc.ca/connexion/tools-outils/temp/visa/intake-reception/place-](http://cicintranet.ci.gc.ca/connexion/tools-outils/temp/visa/intake-reception/place-)

, Orientation du programme d’immigration Immigration, Réfugiés et Citoyenneté Canada / Gouvernement du Canada Ryan.McIntyre@cic.gc.ca / Tél.: 613-437-8899 From: McIntyre.Ryan Sent: November 23, 2016 3:22 PM To: Rushton.Robin-Leah <Robin-Leah.Rushton@cic.gc.ca>; Taljit.Gary <Gary.Taljit@cic.gc.ca>; Balaberda.Cynthia <Cynthia.Balaberda@cic.gc.ca>; Tissot.Angele <Angele.Tissot@cic.gc.ca>; Lemay.Marie-Claire <Marie-Claire.Lemay@cic.gc.ca> Subject: RE: Converting OP1 sections on R11 - "Where to apply"

Good afternoon everyone,

At the moment, the Reg changes lack a clear timeline, but we should know more in the new year. As such, I've spoken to the lead on OP1 conversion and they agreed that we can wait for a few months before proceeding with any PDI changes. If the regs are going to change sooner than later, and with the time delays on PDI updates, we want to avoid going through a long update process only to have the content completely gutted within a few months. If it is looking like the reg changes are going to take a while, then we will pick this back up again and from OP1 to the PDIs.

Thanks again for all your help, and I'll get back to you 2017 with an update.

From: McIntyre.Ryan Sent: October-24-16 3:36 PM To: Rushton.Robin-Leah <Robin-Leah.Rushton@cic.gc.ca>; Taljit.Gary <Gary.Taljit@cic.gc.ca>; Balaberda.Cynthia <Cynthia.Balaberda@cic.gc.ca>; Tissot.Angele <Angele.Tissot@cic.gc.ca>; Lemay.Marie-Claire <Marie-Claire.Lemay@cic.gc.ca> Subject: RE: Converting OP1 sections on R11 - "Where to apply"

Thank you everyone for the comments. I'll pull them together and recirculate an updated draft again prior to submitting for PDI update.

# One Pager: R11 OP1 PDI conversion

Background IRCC old format manuals are being gradually converted to PDIs. Overseas Processing 1 (OP1) is an omnibus manual which is being converted in pieces into subject-specific PDIs. The sections on “Where to Apply”, which operationalizes IRPR Section 11, is being coordinated by TRPD. This had been put on hold for most of 2016 and early 2017,

This is still the plan, but as of April 2017 there is no timeline for this Regulatory change so we are proceeding with the conversion so as not to hold up the eventual complete conversion of OP1.

## Objective

To produce an update to TR PDI pages to move all R11 content from OP1 to the PDIs.

Tasks and Timelines

1. Circulated updated PDI proposal for input from the rest of IPG and the Networks April 2017
### 2. Submit PDI update to APD (who are coordinating OP1 conversions) Summer 2017

Revised May 2017

My apologies for the delay. You may modify the text as you see fit.

8.1 Privacy act. The processing time is included in the guidance for CSC agents. If you deem necessary, it can be added to OP1. A Privacy request must be completed within 30 calendar days from the date of receipt. However, this time limit can be extended for limited and specific reasons defined in the Act. CSC agents consult the following procedure when speaking to clients. [http://cicwebcart/trainingmodules/tools/privacy](http://cicwebcart/trainingmodules/tools/privacy) module/privact-eng.aspx
8.2 Members of Parliament Please note that some constituents located in Canada contact MP offices for applications that are being processed at a mission outside Canada. The processing times mentioned are accurate. For your information, the ministerial enquiry team in Ottawa has the following service standards: 5 days for a written request and 2 days to call back an MP office. We note that the document OP1 is aimed at providing guidance to MPMs in missions abroad.

## Good morning IPG colleagues,

The Publication Coordination Renewal (PCR) team within the Functional Direction Optimization unit has implemented a tracker to plan and track upcoming IPG-led PDI publication requests. This IPG PDI tracker will be sent to you on a quarterly basis as a way to help PCR better prepare for the incoming workload, and plan accordingly among the Communications teams. Let us know what you plan to work on by completing the tracker to the best of your ability. It can include minor updates, new PDI development,

## or large program updates.

Review the attached tracker and input the required data directly in the document below for Q3 by COB, September 16. Please note that if column C is selected as "no", the remaining columns do not require your input.

We appreciate your ongoing support in improving the IPG PDI publication process.

Please do not hesitate to contact us if you have any questions via the following: RCC.IPGPDIPublish-PublierlEPOPI.IRCC@cic.gc.ca

Document Overview: IPG PDI Planning-FY 2022-2023.xlsx (ci.gc.ca)

## 9. Documentretentionand disposal

When deciding whether to retain or dispose of a document on a permanent or temporary residence application, visa offices should consider

whether the document is directly germane to any positive or negative decision made on the application; whether the document may be essential as evidence in any administrative or judicial proceedings resulting from a refusal of the application; whether GCMS notes are sufficiently well documented concerning the material so that it can be disposed of or returned to the applicant; the need to ensure that applications are as thin as possible to ensure efficient space management

## at migration offices.

9.1 Principles guiding the retention and disposal of documents Migration offices have the authority to retain minimum paper documentation on immigrant and non- immigrant files in accordance with the guidelines in section 19.2.] Documentation obtainable from other sources is not retained on finalized files. Examples include copies of permanent resident counterfoils and confirmation of permanent residence, temporary resident permits, Quebec Acceptance Certificates (CAQs) and CSQs. Relevant details from these documents should be recorded in GCMS.
## Migration offices should regularly review the documents that clients submit with their applications to ensure

that only those documents that are relevant to the processing of a case are requested. Should migration offices note that documents routinely requested in the application kits are not relevant to decision-making in a permanent or temporary residence application, they should notify NHQ to allow for a review of kits to determine if those documents should no longer be required.

Migration offices should look for administrative opportunities to dispose of unneeded documents throughout the application process. Where possibie, decisions on retention or disposal of documents should be made only at the finalization of an application.

Digitized documentation on approved and refused files, is only admissible if the

# documentation has been digitized in accordance with IRCC's Digitization Framework.

## Original copies of documentation can only be disposed of if proper digitization

## procedures are followed. Please consult the Digitization Manual for IRCC for detailed

requirements.

## If digitization procedures are not followed, the scanned documentation is inadmissible in

court proceedings under section 31 of the Canada Evidence Act.

There are no legal impediments to immediately scanning documentation on approved and refused files. Scanned documentation is admissible in court proceedings under section 31 of the Canada Evidence Act.

Library and Archives Canada requires that the paper file be retained where an applicant might have been involved in war crimes or crimes against humanity. Five years after finalization, these files are to be forwarded to the Government Archives Division (GAD) of the Library and Archives Canada, 395 Wellington Street, Ottawa, Canada K1A ON3.

### Commented [Y1]: IM Recommendation (General)

Replace the word scan with digitize throughout to be consistent with IRCC's Digitization Framework and to emphasize the process which must be followed for digitized documents to be admissible. Commented [Y2]: IM Recommendation:

Instead of asking offices to make an ad hoc decision whether to retain or dispose, they should be provided with guidelines on what to accept.

### Commented [Y3]: IM Recommendation:

Follow guidance that exists in kits to determine what materials to accept. Commented [C4]: Add hyperlink

Commented [S5]: Recheck this

## Commented [Y6]: Pending confirmation from Library and

Archives Canada.

### 9.2. Guidelines for the retention and disposal of documents

# During processing

Documents that support routine processing are not to be filed. Examples of such documents include birth, marriage and education certificates, and copies of passports in immigrant files; and curricula vitae, school acceptances, statements of net worth, and ietters of invitation in non immigrant files. The date of receipt and nature of contents, where applicable, are to be recorded in GCMS notes.

After their receipt has been recorded, the documents are either discarded or, if they are originals, they are

### returned to the applicant.

The officer's record of information from returned or discarded documents must be sufficiently detailed to permit another officer to continue processing or to respond to representations. Officers must also ensure that GCMS notes record any relevant information that may be used in administrative or judicial proceedings should a refusal be appealed.

Officers may keep contentious or questionable documents on file during processing. If officers rely on aCommented [Y7]: IM Comment: document to refuse an application, they will scan it into GCMS. This is a subjective determination that requires clarification Officers must retain all evidence of an applicant's involvement in war crimes or crimes against humanity. to ensure uniformity across all process officers. Commented [Y8]: IM Recommendation:

### Following finalization

Consult Dept of Justice / DLSU, Case Management, and For those migration offices that have the capability, contents of files may be scanned except for Litigation Management to determine if this guidance can be documents relating to an applicant's involvement in war crimes or crimes against humanity. These more precise in terms of which documents/forms should be documents are to be kept for five years and then forwarded to Library and Árchives Canada. The IMM retained. 0008E form may be scanned except in the case of applications by persons involved in war crimes or crimes against humanity.

Where appropriate, the scanning of documents should immediately follow finalization.

OP1 conversion to PDls is with communications for staging and publication. They are aiming for a publication date of August 18. They will send the staged products approximately August 15 for a final content review before going live. Someone will need to review the documents one more time for content accuracy and give approval to go live. OP1 should be achieved once Integrity Risk Management completes the section on ARC. OP3 to PDIs is with legal for review. My understanding is that SASI will make any required changes and follow up consultations if needed based on legal comments. ### could do a word count (it is a big document).

When the application of the PA in Canada is ready to be finalized and the applicant can become a permanent resident, the CPC or in Canada IRCC will take appropriate steps to grant permanent residence and wellas take the following steps to notify the overseas migration office that permanent residence will be granted to the PA in Canada:

Hi Rennie, Thanks for sharing the updated document on IN strategy. I read it and condensed it. I did not change the mandate. Please let me know what you think of this:

# 5. International Network

## Vision of the International Network

Since 1994, Immigration, Refugees, and Citizenship Canada's (IRCC) mission has been to help build a stronger Canada. International Network supports IRCC in fulfilling the vision of a stronger Canada-a safe and secure country with a shared bond of citizenship and values; a country that continues to support our humanitarian tradition and draws the best from the world to help build a nation that is economically, socially and culturally prosperous.

International Network contributes to this vision by: Leading Canada's migration agenda abroad; Delivering IRCC programs through innovative, client-centric services; and Providing a well-supported workforce to advance IRCC's program and policy goals

A3996662_4-000110

Immigration, Réfugiés Immigration, Refugees and Citizenship Canadaet Citoyenneté Canada Information disclosed under the Access to Information Act L'information divulguée en vertu de la loi sur l’accès à linformation

## Values of the International Network

Four values inform how International Network will bring this vision to life as well as to inform the decisions it makes, large and small:

### Client-centred

# Employee-focussed

### Innovative Accountable

International Network delivers operational excellence and is continuously making improvements to the effectiveness and efficiency of IRCC while ensuring the needs of clients are met. Employees have the knowledge, the skills and the tools to do their job while ensuring a high standard of program integrity; guarding Canada's health, safety and security; meeting the economic and social needs of Canada; and

#### ensuring public funds are spent wisely.

#### Mandate of the International Network

The International Network delivers immigration programs abroad by:

•selecting foreign nationals and refugees in accordance with the Government's plans and

#### policies • facilitating the admission to Canada of genuine visitors for tourism, studies and temporary

employment; contributing to the development of Canada's migration, refugee and social policies by reporting and analysing international trends and developments in these fields; advising on the foreign policy implications of domestic policies and on the impact of international migration trends in the domestic arena; and •maintaining liaison with foreign governments, international agencies and non- governmental organizations

### 5. International Network

The International Network delivers immigration programs abroad and is also the lead for Canada's migration agenda internationally. In support of IRCC's mandate, International Network has three main areas of focus:

A3996666_1-000114

Immigration, Refugees and Citizenship Canadaet Citoyenneté Canada L'information divulguée en vertu de la loi sur l'accès à l’information

鲁Business and risk intelligence Program delivery and stewardship Global engagement

5.1 Mandate of the International Network The International Network delivers on its mandate abroad by:
- selecting foreign nationals and refugees in accordance with the Government's plans and policies contributing to the facilitation of bona fide applications, the recruitment of migrants and the identification of risks by gathering and validating information, assessing risk, identifying trends, providing analysis and managing relationships to support decision-making and information products across the IRCC integrated network; •facilitating the admission to Canada of genuine visitors for tourism, studies and temporary employment; •maintaining liaison with foreign governments, international agencies and non-governmental organizations
- contributing to the development of Canada's migration, refugee and social policies by reporting and analysing international trends and developments in these fields;
- advising on the foreign policy implications of domestic policies and on the impact of international migration trends in the domestic arena; representing Canada and advocating for Canada's interests on the full range of issues within the IRCC mandate, including migration and refugee protection, settlement and integration, citizenship and passport program; leading the management of relationships with foreign governments, international organizations and non-governmental organizations working in areas within the IRCC mandate; promoting and delivering targeted outreach to best meet Government of Canada priorities for recruiting global talent, regional economic development and minority linguistic community needs.
5.2 Principles of the International Network International Network achieves excellence in fulfilling its mandate and realizing its vision through a shared commitment to: professionalism; client service; integrity; accountability; innovation; teamwork; fairness and compassion
5.3 Vision of the International Network

# International Network contributes to Canada's cultural diversity and to its economic prosperity and growth through

## innovative, cost-effective and responsible management of Canadian immigration programs abroad. International Network

strives to achieve excellence in providing services which meet the diverse and changing needs of clients, partner agencies and the Canadian public.

As the principal representatives abroad of Canadian interests in the fields of migration and protection diplomacy ef international migration, refugee and social policy issues, Ns officers are advocates of Canadian values and practices in the delivery of immigration programs. Officers also play a crucial role in maintaining the safety and order of Canadian society by ensuring that individuals who pose security or criminal risks are denied entry to Canada. ***

Good afternoon colleagues, The first draft of the updated OP1 is ready for your review. I tried to narrow the focus for colleagues who need to review specific sections however please feel free to provide feedback if you have time and would like to review the entire manual. You will note that rather than repeat information that is already published as PDls or in a manual a link is provided to that information. Communications advise that we should not repeat the info instead we should provide the link.

As the head of Canada's delegation to the Special Commission Meeting for the Operation of the Hague Intercountry Adoption Convention (July 4 - 8), Marie (CAILS) feels it is impossible to meet the deadline and that this review (processing guidance and the adoption legal/policy information) is very important to get right. RE: please consult with GAC on this portion of adoption PDIs

Would you be so kind as to reach out to GAC to see if they are ok with the passage below and if so could they provide an

Child trafficking and undue gain contravene the laws of most countries. All adoptions should be undertaken in accordance with the intent and spirit of the Hague Convention, even if an adoption is from a country that has not ratified the Convention. If an officer has evidence that child trafficking has taken place or that there was undue gain in the process (a child was sold or improper financial gain took place), the officer should refuse the case on the basis that the requirements were not met according to the spirit and intent of the Hague Convention (see subparagraph R117(1)(g)(ii), clause

Additionally, the officer should inform the appropriate local authorities of the criminal activities (if applicable) and advise

the Canadian Federal Central Authority for the 1980 Convention on the Civil Aspects of International Child

## Procedure

1. Kickoff meeting with content owner.
a. Discuss where their content is at in terms of updates they are working on.
2. Come up with a content architecture. Keep in mind that some content may belong elsewhere (i.e. not in your section). Consider the PDls holistically, not just the chapter you've been given.
### 3. Get the content architecture approved by content owner.

4. Content dump into architecture (EN). This means copy/paste existing content into separate word documents per the new structure. (Usually done by IPGB, but depends on who has time).
## 5. Editing PDIs (consolidating them, removing duplication and unnecessary language, flagging

things that are unclear). Usually done by IPGB or both, depending on who has time.

6. Web recommendations (proper heading levels, ensure links on descriptive text, etc.) PDI content (EN only).
7. Get EN content (PDI) reviewed/approved by client.
a. Provide a note for the content owner in the email stating that they should do a thorough read-through ensuring we did not miss anything. Give them a clear deadline and remind them that this is their chance to make changes (not on staging). Ask them to use track changes mode.
8. Prepare to delete or update (if required) manual or section of the manual.
a. Manual or section of the manual are usually PDF. You need to ask NHQ-Manual-Editor for the master word version to make the edits.
b.Reminder when deleting you must delete the actual manual and update the broken links (excluding OB) to the deleted manual.
i. Ask web ops to provide you a list of pages that link to the manual being deleted.
9. Send to translation. If recycling French, need to do a content dump of equivalent French, then ask TB to make the changes they see in track changes mode in the EN document. (Web Advisor sends to CommsTranslation email address and uses the translation request form)
### a. PDI (full translation)

## b. Ask for someone that knows the subject matter.

c. If more document to come let TB know.

b. Get manual editor to verify the titles only.
11. Insert coding instructions in EN and FR PDI documents.
12. Send to web ops for coding.

# Manual modernization process

1. Usability review of PDI English content
2. Build transition table that lists where: Content was (chapter and section / OB number) It can now be found (hyperlinked breadcrumbs) for INT and EXT
3. Client approval of PDI English content & transition table Ask that they edit using track changes and comments o Give them a clear deadline o Remind them this is their chance to make changes (not on staging)
4. Manual updates: Ask NHQ-Manual-Editor for the master word version to make the edits Delete the section Enter text that directs users to the new PDI pages (ENG and FRE) If the whole manual is being deleted:
request a list of backlinks from WebOps update the broken links to direct to the new PDIs send a request to delete the actual manual (when coding)

5. Draft English internal announcements Create a PDU with IPGB Send the EN to NHQ-Manual-Editor for editing
6. Send to translation (PDI, Transition table and PDU) o List "Manual Modernization" as the "File documents relate to:" project in the form. If recycling French: ■ do a content dump of equivalent French ask TB to make changes they see in track changes in the EN o If doing a full translation, ask for someone who knows the subject matter. When you get it back from translation:
■ Check to see if it was translated appropriately, including same number of paragraphs, headings, links, nothing was missed. Get manual editor to verify the titles. Insert coding instructions in EN and FR documents.

7. Send to WebOps for coding ○PDIs o Forms (if necessary) Manual update Contacts (if necessary) 。 PDU Glossary (if necessary) 0 Transition table Expired OB (if required) 。TOC
8. Quality assurance Send staging link to client for review/approval. Once approved, ask Web Ops to go live.
9. Ask NHQ-Manual-Editor to prepare and send the blue sky email.
10. Copy the live links and sent to OMC-Webcart-Kits for archiving.

## How to publish or update program

### delivery instructions (PDIs)

When you need to publish or update PDls, please let the PDls web advisors know about your project as early as possible. This will ensure publication targets are realistic. Be sure to copy your manager on the email. Urgent requests: If your request is urgent, please explain why in your email and provide director-level approval for the rush posting.

##### IPG and COVID-19 PDIs

The coordination of all IPG PDIs and COVID-19 PDIs is now being done through the Integrated Coordination Unit, Social Affairs and Strategic Initiatives Division, Immigration Program Guidance Branch (ICU-SASI-IPG).

Please send your heads up and publishing requests to IRCC.IPGPDIPublish-PublierlEPOPI.IRCC@cic.gc.ca.

[http://cicintranet.ci.gc.ca/connexion/tools-outils/authors-auteurs/PDl-IEP-fra.aspx](http://cicintranet.ci.gc.ca/connexion/tools-outils/authors-auteurs/PDl-IEP-fra.aspx)

#### Comment publier ou mettre à jour les instructions sur l'exécution des

programmes (IEP) Quand vous devez publier ou mettre à jour les lEP, veuillez informer les conseillers Web pour les lEP de votre projet le plus tôt possible. De cette façon, les objectifs de publication seront réalistes. Assurez-vous d’envoyer une copie conforme de votre courriel à votre gestionnaire.

# SASI-ICU: Coordinating

### New steps for PDI authors – official launch November 19, 2020

Consultations Submit to ICU forApprove links to Draft PDI/PDU PDF creation Heads up to ICU Director approval (including with ICU) publication go live

### What is changing? Why is the ICU coordinating PDI publications?

In January 2020, Internal Communications approached IPG-DGO with the need for: ICU will maintain a branch-wide list of PDIs being drafted for DGO review. ICU will be consulted on the final draft of PDIs; we will review for high-level usability to guidance on prioritization of requests ensure the guidance is clear for officers. faster, continuous archiving ICU will correspond with the following groups to help free-up your advisors' time: updates to the PDI owners matrix o Internal Communications to ensure the document is written to meet web standards coordination of large-scale redesign projects o the Translation Bureau for the translation of PDIs and PDl-related products (PDUs, branch-wide consideration of the service level agreements between template letters, etc.) Communications and IPG for the publication of PDIs Othe Editing Unit for final proofreading and French comparative edits Director approval of the PDIs will be for the validity of the instructions only. Changes will What is staying the same? often be applied by Communications to the formatting, layout and style of the PDI, after IPG units must continue to: approval. SASl-Director will authorize publishing priorities based on a number of factors, including: keep their PDIs up to date o release date of policy draft new PDIs and perform proper consultations opublic announcements by the Communications Branch receive director-level approvals on PDIs Osize and complexity of the update distinguish between officer instructions and instructions for clients, and work At the end of the PDI process, IPG units will be responsible for saving their newly-published with their Strategic Communications Advisor to develop and update their PDI (or updated PDl), as a PDF copy of the webpage for archiving purposes. public web pages for clients

Transition tools Why is saving a PDF version now a part of the process for IPG units?

Once a web page is updated, the previous version of the page is irretrievable. IRCC must archive PDIs for ATIP requests and court cases ICU has created: PDI owners are asked to save PDF copies of their instructions as soon as the PDIs are published live. This will ensure that a web page does A step-by-step procedures for PDI not become overwritten. ICU will continue to maintain the PDI archives by: authors Templates for PDI and PDU drafts renaming PDF files appropriately to be able to find them in the archives A web writing tips guide moving the files to their proper subfolders in the GCDOCs archives finding the files when a request for a previous PDI version comes in mm g 40on. ## Ce qui change Pourquoi l’UCl coordonne-t-elle la publication des lEP?

En janvier 2020, les communications internes ont approché le BDG de la DGOPI

- L'UCI tiendra, à l’échelle de la Direction générale, une liste des IEP rédigées et soumises au BDG
concernant les besoins suivants : pour examen.

- L'UCI sera consultée relativement à la version définitive des lEP; elle procédera à une révision pour • orientation sur l’établissement de l’ordre de priorité des demandes; assurer une grande convivialité, afin que l’orientation soit claire pour les agents. • archivage continu et plus rapide;
- L'UCl communiquera avec les groupes suivants afin d’alléger la tâche des conseillers : • mise à jour de la matrice des responsables des lEP; o les Communications internes, pour que le document soit rédigé selon les normes du Web; • coordination de projets de restructuration à grande échelle; o le Bureau de la traduction, pour la traduction des lEP et des produits connexes (mises à jour, lettres • examen, par l’ensemble de la direction générale, des ententes sur les niveaux de modèles, etc.);
service entre les Communications et la DGOPI pour la publication des IEP. o l’Unité de révision, pour la correction d’épreuve finale et la modification comparative du français.

- L'approbation des lEP par le directeur servira uniquement à valider les instructions. Les
### Ce qui ne change pas?

Communications apporteront souvent des changements au formatage, à la mise en page et au style Les unités de la DGOPI doivent continuer à : de l’IEP après l’approbation.

- tenir à jour leurs IEP;
- Le directeur d’ASIS autorisera les priorités de publication en fonction de plusieurs facteurs, dont :
- rédiger de nouvelles IEP et mener les consultations adéquates;
o la date de publication de la politique;

- recevoir des approbations de la direction concernant les lEP;
o les annonces publiques de la Direction générale des communications;

- faire la distinction entre les instructions à l’intention des agents et celles à l’intention des
o la taille et complexité de la mise à jour. clients et collaborer avec leur conseiller en communication stratégique pour créer et

- À la fin du processus d’IEP, les unités de la DGOPI seront responsables de la sauvegarde de leur IEP
mettre à jour leurs pages Web publiques pour les clients. nouvellement publiée (ou mise à jour) ainsi que d’une copie PDF de leur page aux fins d’archivage.

### Outils de transition Pourquoi les unités de la DGOPI doivent-elles désormais sauvegarder une version PDF?

L'UCI a créé : Lorsqu’une page Web est mise à jour, sa version antérieure devient inaccessible. IRCC doit archiver les IEP pour les demandes d’AIPRP et les cas portés devant les tribunaux.

- Une procédure étape par étape pour les
On demande aux responsables des IEP de sauvegarder une copie PDF de leurs instructions dès la publication des IEP en ligne. On évitera ainsi auteurs d'IEP;

- Des gabarits pour la rédaction d'lEP et d’écraser une page Web. L'UCI poursuivra la tenue à jour des archives des IEP, qui comprend les tâches suivantes : de mises à jour; • renommer les fichiers PDF comme il se doit afin de pouvoir les retrouver dans les archives;
- Un guide contenant des trucs pour la • déplacer les fichiers dans les bons sous-fichiers des archives GCdocs; rédaction Web. • trouver les fichiers lorsque la version antérieure d'une lEP est demandée. mmg aon Retgee s mmgeton, Reatges and Ctzensnp Canada t Otoye mete Cana ca

# PDI Coordination Transfer

## Immigration Program Guidance and Internal

### Communications

Immigration, Refugees Immigration, Réfugiés A4066110_1-000182 and Čitizenship Canada et Citoyenneté Canada

mmigration, Refugees Immigration, Réfugiés and Citizenship Canadaet Citoyenneté Canada Information disclosed under the Access to Information Act L'information divulguée en vertu de la loi sur l'accès à l’information

# Background

IRCC began the conversion of the Manuals into HTML format (the “Manual Modernization Project”), in 2013. There are currently 4710 operational guidance files on Connexion. The majority of these files are owned in whole or in part by IPG.

In January 2020, Internal Communications approached IPG-DGO with the following concerns:

- Need for guidance on prioritization of requests.
- Need for continuous archiving of Program Delivery Updates (PDIs). This work stopped when Webcart transferred to CEB. Need to update the Manuals owner matrix Multiple Units within IPG were requesting larger scale redesign projects without consulting first within their own branch. With the significant increase in the number of requests, Internal Communications does not have the capacity to undertake new redesign projects. Requests for urgent publications were regularly being submitted to Internal Communications without taking into account the Service Level Agreements of the Editing and Coding units.
# Solution

To mitigate these pain points, and to ensure a more centralized publication process within IPG:

- Integrated Coordination Unit (ICU) will lead the publication coordination of operational guidance (PDIs, OBs, Manuals) owned, in full or in part, by IPG. Internal Communications (IC) will focus on web coordination including usability testing, Information Architecture, navigation and coordinating coding. Both Editing and Coding units keep the same role in this process.
Once this new process is in place, ICU will coordinate all new requests. Existing active requests will be completed by Internal Communications as per the existing process.

## The new publication process

The general publication flow is outlined in Annex A The following detailed process outlines the new publication flow as well as the responsibilities of the corresponding units:

1. A need for update or new program delivery instructions (PDI) is identified and assigned to the responsible Unit in IPG (“IPG Author”)
2. IPG Author gives heads up to ICU
3. ICU creates a request number and centralized GCDOCS folder
4. ICU gives heads up to Internal Communications (IC) and Strategic Comms to alert them to upcoming program delivery instructions
5. IPG Author drafts the PDI and PDU (program delivery update)
A4066110_2-000183

mmigration, Refugees and Citizenship Canadaet Citoyenneté Canada L'information divulguée en vertu de la loi sur l'accès à l’information

6. IPG Author consults on their draft with all operations authorities, including ICU for:
a. a quality control review on the document (e.g. proposal for language to enhance users' comprehension)
# b. guidance for PDI look and feel (e.g. recommendations for best location of the

PDIs, formatting, etc.)

7. IPG Author gets Director approval on the PDI and PDU, for content accuracy and validation
8. IPG Author submits Director-approved, valid English (EN) documents to ICU
9. ICU sends to IC for a usability review including conformance with TBS and IRCC web publication standards. IC will provide comments and advice on content layout if needed.
10. ICU sends final, approved EN doc to Editing Unit for an EN proofread
11. IPG Author conducts a final review on the formatting, writing, and editing revisions made by ICU and Communications
12. ICU sends EN doc for translation to have a French (FR) document created
13. ICU sends FR doc to Editing Unit for a FR comparative edit
14. ICU sends final EN and FR docs to IC for coding
15. IC coordinates with the Coding team
16. IC sends staging links to IPG Author with ICU cc'ed, for review of the staged material
17. IPG Author gives approval to go live
18. IC ensures DGO COMMS provides the green light to publish
19. IC asks the coding team to publish to the live website(s) and sends live links to IPG author and ICU
20. IPG author saves PDF versions the newly-live links and ICU adds the PDFs to the PDI archives
21. IC sends out the PDU email once pages are live
22. ICU files all associated emails and documents in GCDOCs related to the request
Additional duties outside the general PDI content updates

Internal Communications and ICU must be consulted as early as possible in the event of:

- revamp of existing sections of PDIs
- creation of a new section
- updates to the PDI external and internal landing pages
- updates to the mega menu or the left navigation panels
Internal Communications, as web expert, are responsible for:

Internet Architecture specifications such as Organizing content and Designing content

- Usability Testing of new designs
- Web Writing best practices
- Digital tools to support business needs

Subject: For approval and signature: PDI Coordination Transfer agreement

# Hi Jon,

As you know, there have been ongoing discussions with IPG to revise the roles and responsibilities between the Connexion PDIs advisors and the new IPG PDI coordination team. IPG is now asking for you and Jack to sign an agreement reflecting the revised roles and responsibilities.

Recommendation: Approve and sign. Toufic and I are comfortable with the process and the division of roles & responsibilities laid out in this document.

### Considerations:

Connexion team role is limited to usability reviews/advice and coding requests for PDIs owned by IPG (~ 2/3rd of all PDIs). Connexion team still the coordinator for all other PDI including usability reviews, document management, editing and coding requests. Toufic and his team have worked with IPG to develop more detailed process and tracking documents that support this roles &responsibilities agreement Toufic asked IPG to consult with the Editing team before they send to Jack for approval. They should have done so this week.

To: IPG ALL Subject: New PDI procedures

Hello IPG colleagues,

The ICU is officially launching the new PDI publication process for the Branch. IPG Directors have already received a summary placemat of what is changing. We have taken into consideration input from our consultations with Assistant Directors.

As of today, please follow these steps for writing and posting PDIs:

- PDI publication – Steps for IPG
If you have a request already with Internal Web (COMM PDI) The Internal Web team will continue to coordinate and process requests from IPG that have already been submitted to them and given a request number. ICU will be starting a new format for request numbers.

PDIs in draft format As part of the new process, we are asking for a heads-up on PDls (either updates or new pages) at the moment they are assigned to be drafted. But, we need your support to get our list going. Email us a list of PDIs Send us a list of all your draft PDIs following the below steps:

1) write to IRCC.IPGPDIPublish-PublierlEPOPI.IRCC@cic.gc.ca
2) copy your Assistant Director to the email
3) fill out the following table in your email for each PDI publication request* you are creating

Why is saving a PDF version now a part of the process for IPG units?

Once a web page is updated, the previous version of the page is irretrievable. IRCC must archive PDIs for ATIP requests and legislative purposes.

PDI owners are asked to save PDF copies of their instructions as soon as the PDIs are published live. This will ensure that a web page doesn't become overwritten. ICU will continue to maintain the PDI archives by:

- renaming PDF files appropriately to be able to find them in the archives moving the files to their proper subfolders in the GCDOCs archives
- finding the files when a request for a previous PDI version comes in

À : Tous les employés de la DGOPI Objet : Nouvelles procédures aux termes des IEP

Bonjour chers collègues de la DGOPI,

L'UCI lance officiellement le nouveau processus de publication d’IEP pour la Direction générale. Les directeurs de la DGOPI ont déjà reçu un récapitulatif de ce qui change. Nous avons pris en considération les observations issues de nos consultations avec les directeurs adjoints.

À compter d’aujourd'hui, veuillez suivre les étapes suivantes pour la rédaction et l’affichage des

# IEP:

- Publication des IEP-Étapes pour la DGOPI
Si vous avez déjà une demande auprès des Communications internes (COMM PDI) L’équipe du Web interne continuera de coordonner et de traiter les demandes de la DGOPI qui lui ont déjà été soumises et auxquelles un numéro de demande a été attribué. L'UCl va mettre en place un nouveau format pour les numéros de demande.

Mais nous avons besoin de votre appui pour mettre notre liste sur pied. Pourquoi la sauvegarde d’une version PDF fait-elle désormais partie du processus pour les unités de la DGOPI?

Une fois qu'une page Web est mise à jour, la version antérieure de la page est irrécupérable. IRCC doit archiver les IEP pour les demandes d’AIPRP et pour les fins législatives. Les responsables d’IEP sont priés de sauvegarder des copies PDF de leurs instructions dès que les lEP sont publiées. Cela permettra de veiller à ce qu’une page Web ne soit pas écrasée. L’UCI continuera d’assurer la maintenance des archives d’IEP :

- en renommant les fichiers PDF de façon appropriée pour pouvoir les retrouver dans les archives;
- en déplaçant les fichiers vers les sous-répertoires appropriés dans les archives GCDOC; en trouvant les fichiers lorsqu'une demande de version antérieure d’IEP est présentée.

# PDI process: Current

1) Program writes factually-correct draft of PDI
2) Sends to ICU which sends to Internal web team
3) Review by internal web to confirm publication ready
4) Back to program for Director approval (this step has been combined with step 1 for recent urgent publications, thus the process hasn't been going as planned)
5) Program sends approved copy to SASI
6) SASI sends to the translation bureau, for translation
7) SAS! sends both English and French versions to Internal Web, and cc's a strat advisor
8) Internal Web sends for an English edit and comparative French edit
9) Internal Web liaises between Editing and SASI
10) Internal Web sends to the coding (publishing) team for posting online

# PDI process: Proposed for optimization

1) Programs write factually-correct draft of PDI following the “Web-writing tips and best practices" appendix in the IPG procedures for publishing PDIs
2) Programs send draft PDI and PDU to SASI for
a)setting up a request number and tracking info appropriately
b)review/reworking to confirm draft is publication ready
3) SASI sends a heads-up to Strat Comms and Internal Web, and returns PDI package to program
4) Director-approval by program
5)Program sends approved copy to SASI
6) SASI sends for English to Editing Unit for English proofread
7) SASI sends English edited version to the translation bureau, for translation
8) SASI sends translated version to Editing Unit for comparative edit
9)SASI sends final copies to the Internal Web team for posting. > Need to create in a presentable document to share with all ADs (IPG and Comms)

instructions

# Communicating with Comms: Explanation of

groups Program delivery decisions made in IPG affect 2 types of audiences:

1) External clients – people who need to know how to come to Canada
2) Internal officers – people who need to know how to process the clients who want to come Canada. There are 2 different groups in comms for either audience:
1) Strategic comms (and External Web) - for our external clients
2) Internal Web Comms – for our internal officers

# PDI coordination optimization activities-December to March 31

The following steps outline the activities required to optimize the PDI coordination function for IPG to formally respond to and solve the concerns brought up by the Communications Branch in January 2020.

Step 1 – Move coordination to IPG

Set up an easy-to-follow administrative process and officially launch to the Branch (complete) Train members on the team on the PDI and how to manage the IPG PDI Pub box Move master manuals folder from Comms and train team on their processing Update the Owner's matrix so all of Operations and Comms knows which instructions are owned by which groups, to maintain the pages

## Step 2 – Support IPG with training and training materials

- Training session 1) The Ins and Outs of PDIs: What they are and how they are published 0 Develop material Hold 1 to 1.5 hour sessions with all units (January-February)
- Training session 2) Web writing basics: Setting up PDIs for easy reading The purpose of the PDU and what to put in it (background etc) The importance of headings and hyperlinks Avoid repetition o General overview of the Web writing tips document Hold 1 to 1.5 hour sessions with all units (February-March) •Rewriting content on author pages as agreed in Annex B of the PDI Coordination transfer document including: oOverview of the Immigration Program Guidance Branch o Consolidation of IPG mailboxes oAuthor tools and its subpages

previous publication system

# PDI Coordination Transfer

## Immigration Program Guidance and Internal

### Communications

#### A4066140_1-000210

Immigration, Refugees Immigration, Réfugiés Canada

mmigration, Refugees Immigration, Réfugiés and Citizenship Canadaet Citoyenneté Canada Information disclosed under the Access to Information Act L'information divulguée en vertu de la loi sur l'accès à l’information

# Background

IRCC began the conversion of the Manuals into HTML format (the “Manual Modernization Project”), in 2013. There are currently 4710 operational guidance files on Connexion. The majority of these files are owned in whole or in part by IPG.

In January 2020, Internal Communications approached IPG-DGO with the following concerns:

- Need for guidance on prioritization of requests. Need for continuous archiving of Program Delivery Updates (PDIs). This work stopped when Webcart transferred to CEB. Need to update the Manuals owner matrix Multiple Units within IPG were requesting larger scale redesign projects without consulting first within their own branch. With the significant increase in the number of requests, Internal Communications does not have the capacity to undertake new redesign projects. Requests for urgent publications were regularly being submitted to Internal Communications without taking into account the Service Level Agreements of the Editing and Coding units.
# Solution

To mitigate these pain points, and to ensure a more centralized publication process within IPG:

- Integrated Coordination Unit (ICU) will lead the publication coordination of operational guidance (PDIs, OBs, Manuals) owned, in full or in part, by IPG. Internal Communications (IC) will focus on web coordination including usability testing, Information Architecture, navigation and coordinating coding. Both Editing and Coding units keep the same role in this process.
Once this new process is in place, ICU will coordinate all new requests. Existing active requests will be completed by Internal Communications as per the existing process.

## The new publication process

The general publication flow is outlined in Annex A The following detailed process outlines the new publication flow as well as the responsibilities of the corresponding units:

1. A need for update or new program delivery instructions (PDI) is identified and assigned to the responsible Unit in IPG (“IPG Author”)
2. IPG Author gives heads up to ICU
3. ICU creates a request number and centralized GCDOCS folder
4. ICU gives heads up to Internal Communications (IC) and Strategic Comms to alert them to upcoming program delivery instructions
5. IPG Author drafts the PDI and PDU (program delivery update)
A4066140_2-000211

mmigration, Refugees and Citizenship Canadaet Citoyenneté Canada L'information divulguée en vertu de la loi sur l'accès à l’information

6. IPG Author consults on their draft with all operations authorities, including ICU for:
a. a quality control review on the document (e.g. proposal for language to enhance users' comprehension)
# b. guidance for PDI look and feel (e.g. recommendations for best location of the

PDIs, formatting, etc.)

7. IPG Author gets Director approval on the PDI and PDU, for content accuracy and validation
8. IPG Author submits Director-approved, valid English (EN) documents to ICU
9. ICU sends to IC for a usability review including conformance with TBS and IRCC web publication standards. IC will provide comments and advice on content layout if needed.
10. ICU sends final, approved EN doc to Editing Unit for an EN proofread
11. IPG Author conducts a final review on the formatting, writing, and editing revisions made by ICU and Communications
12. ICU sends EN doc for translation to have a French (FR) document created
13. ICU sends EN and FR docs to Editing Unit for a FR comparative edit
14. ICU sends final EN and FR docs to IĆ for coding
15. IC coordinates with the Coding team
16. IC sends staging links to IPG Author with ICU cc'ed, for review of the staged material
17. IPG Author gives approval to go live
18. IC ensures DGO COMMS provides the green light to publish
19. IC asks the coding team to publish to the live website(s) and sends live links to IPG author and ICU
20. IPG author saves PDF versions the newly-live links and ICU adds the PDFs to the PDI archives
21. IC sends out the PDU email once pages are live
22. ICU files all associated emails and documents in GCDOCs related to the request
Additional duties outside the general PDI content updates

Internal Communications and ICU must be consulted as early as possible in the event of:

- revamp of existing sections of PDIs
- creation of a new section
- updates to the PDI external and internal landing pages
- updates to the mega menu or the left navigation panels
Internal Communications, as web expert, are responsible for:

Internet Architecture specifications such as Organizing content and Designing content

- Usability Testing of new designs
- Web Writing best practices
- Digital tools to support business needs
A4066140_3-000212

For any revamp projects or new PDI sections, Internal Communications will work with IPG (ICU Xand the PDI Hub) to build a project plan to ensure all project needs will be met and to fully outline the roles and responsibilities of each Unit.

## UPDATES / RESPONSE TO DGO/ADMO/DM/MINO

Issue / Question Raised: An optimized process for IPG to lead and coordinate the publication of Program Delivery Instructions (PDIs).

# Background • 2013:

The Manual Modernization project began with OMC and Communications to convert over 2000 pages of PDF manuals into web content (the PDIs). Resources were given to Communications (Internal Communications and the Editing Unit) through an MOU for the maintenance of the PDIs, once modernized. The project was led by APD for Ops, but once the Syrian Crisis hit, there was no longer an Ops lead, and Communications became the central point of contact. The number of PDI instructions since that time has grown by 150% (5000+ web pages currently).

- 2017: o PDI owners complained about the process being too slow to meet demands
(publication took 6 months+). Internal Communications (Comms) reviewed the process to optimize the PDI flow within Communications. This service level agreement, while successfully speeding up the process to meet 95% of requested publication dates, put a lot more coordination work on the Internal Comms team. Without an Operations group to coordinate and prioritize the publication requests, Internal Comms had to not only coordinate, but liaise between several different groups in IPG. This caused Internal Comms to be unable to properly web write the PDIs (the “usability review") for an optimal user experience. Deadlines were met, but at the expense of an easy-to-read online PDI (which was part of the purpose of the Manual Modernization project).

- 2020: oComms received requests from several groups within IPG (particularly TRPD)
requesting a full revamp of their PDIs, both structure and presentation. Internal Comms has no resources to meet this demand. OComms met with IPG to raise concerns with the increase in PDI requests and publication timelines and the Communication's service standards were implemented. At this time, IPG announced that SASI was being created and that this Division will be a future partner for Comms in issues related to PDI publication. oDue to COVID-19 ICU created the COVID Operational Bulletin (OB: 669), in an unprecedented collaboration between Comms and IPG this OB became a multiple-page COVID PDI which SASI ICU is currently in charge of maintaining

A4066151_1-000222

mmigration, Refugees and Citizenship Canadaet Citoyenneté Canada Information disclosed under the Access to Information Act L'information divulguée en vertu de la loi sur l’accès à linformation

### UPDATES / RESPONSE TO DGO/ADMO/DM/MINO

# Current Status

- Most PDI requests (particularly COVID ones) include publication needs well ahead of Communications service standards.
- Comms agreed on a one-year assignment for a PDI expert to join the ICU to support the coordination of IPG PDIs and streamline the publication process. This highlights SASI ICU's commitment to enhance the PDI publication experience and increase readership from officers in the field. In July, 2020, the ICU agreed in becoming the centre of expertise for publication of all the PDIs issued by IPG. This innovative approach will include the coordination of regular PDIs, including the COVID-related ones, so that the web advisors can properly conduct usability reviews of the PDIs to maintain an optimal user experience. Meetings have already taken place between ICU, Internal Communications and the Editing Unit and all input has been appropriated into this new, optimized plan. (See Annex A: Process flow)
- An IPG PDI Publications general mailbox is already set-up and ready to receive requests.
## Considerations

- Risks: The ICU-led coordination of the IPG PDIs is currently the best proposed model considering the resources available. The PDI coordination by ICU provides a centre of expertise allowing for: the proper prioritization of the PDI publications confirmation of the signing of MINO documents so that PDIs are not published
prematurely a more timely publication of urgent PDI requests

- ICU currently has more resources available to devote to the coordination of the PDIs than Comms does as per the service level agreement established in 2017.
- Comms liaises with the Editing Unit (part of Strategic Communications) as part of the current PDI publication process. This creates an unnecessary step in the email trail, as Comms is not equipped to answer many of the Editor's questions, which ICU can answer directly. Furthermore, Comms does not have the authority to say when editing may be skipped, or rushed, to meet a publication deadline. As the owners of the PDIs, IPG may make these decisions for timeliness and request a post-publication edit. In the last 3 years, the usability review has not been done to its full potential for all urgent PDIs nor for most PDIs this year since the COVID-19 pandemic started. Increasing the quality of the PDIs would require more Comms Web Advisors revising the material during the drafting stage, to shape the PDIs for a proper online reading (the “usability review" which should be a revision of the PDIs into a proper plain-language, easy-to-scan documents). Mitigation strategies: COVID-19 has and will continue to affect all PDIs, therefore a centralization of intake of the PDIs led by the Branch is essential, which means:

## UPDATES / RESPONSE TO DGO/ADMO/DM/MINO

Eventually, all PDI updates will start to be COVID updates and will come through ICU, and the Unit needs to have a solid plan to manage and maintain the requests while keeping management appraised of the status of their products. The maintenance of a separate COVID-only section of PDIs will be unsustainable while the old versions of the PDIs remain intact. It can cause officer confusion and implementation of incorrect processes if the officers use the Connexion search bar to navigate to a regular PDI without realizing a COVID PDI has the most up-to-date information

- ICU's coordination:
The proposed PDI process is scalable and flexible. As long as the general Standard Operating Procedures (SOPs) are followed, this process will organize and standardize the influx of requests without slowing it down. OThe ICU will leverage the expertise of the Comms PDI expert (Jacqueline Lefebvre) to set up the SOPs and develop onboarding material to train the authors in IPG on the proposed PDI process.

Recommendations

We recommend that you approve, the ICU launching the new and optimized process for PDI publication in IPG, which includes:

- A detailed “Publication steps" document for IPG PDI owners. Including an appendix with web writing tips to help support the proper drafting
for an online environment, since the usability review cannot be done in full at the moment.

- A “heads up" email and tracking sheet so ICU can get the necessary information early on to prepare Comms for any rush publications.
- Updates to the Connexion page for how to publish PDIs.
# Next Steps

- Archiving expired PDIs. The archiving step is currently done through the IPG Functional Guidance mailbox. The ICU will develop a new model to archiving to provide automation of some steps and an improved search capacity. Internal SOPs developed for ICU to maintain the fast-coordination procedures to ensure a smooth publication of the PDIs, with templates in place where necessary.
- Meetings with Strategic Communications Assistant Directors to confirm how ICU should provide the PDIs heads-up to ensure MINO is aware of the PDIs being published.
- Meetings with the Directors in Communications (Strategic Communications, and E- Communications) to discuss a new MOU and potential new resources for the publication of PDIs.

# UCI d'ASIS : Coordination de la publication des IEP

## Nouvelles étapes pour les auteurs d'IEP – entrée en vigueur officielle le 25 novembre 2020

Créer l'ébauche de Mener des Obtenir FIEP/de la mise à consultations (y Prévenir l’UCI l’approbation du directeur jour compris avec l'UCI)

## Ce qui change

L'UCI tiendra, à l’échelle de la Direction générale, une liste des IEP rédigées et soumises au BDG pour examen. L'UCI sera consultée relativement à la version définitive des IEP; elle procédera à une révision pour assurer une grande convivialité, afin que l’orientation soit claire pour les agents. L'UCl communiquera avec les groupes suivants afin d'alléger la tâche des conseillers : oles Communications internes, pour que le document soit rédigé selon les normes du

### Web;

o le Bureau de la traduction, pour la traduction des lEP et des produits connexes (mises à jour, lettres modèles, etc.); ol’Unité de révision, pour la correction d’épreuve finale et la modification comparative du français. L’approbation des lEP par le directeur servira uniquement à valider les instructions. Les Communications apporteront souvent des changements au formatage, à la mise en page et au style de l’IEP après l’approbation. Le directeur d’ASIS autorisera les priorités de publication en fonction de plusieurs facteurs, dont : o la date de publication de la politique; o les annonces publiques de la Direction générale des communications; ola taille et complexité de la mise à jour. À la fin du processus d’IEP, les unités de la DGOPI seront responsables de la sauvegarde de leur IEP nouvellement publiée (ou mise à jour) ainsi que d’une copie PDF de leur page aux fins d’archivage.

Une procédure étape par étape les cas portés devant les tribunaux.

d'IEP et de mises à jour; Un guide contenant des trucs pour la rédaction Web.

Approuver les liens Soumettre à FUCl pour la publication Créer un PDF pour publication en ligne

### Pourquoi l’UCI coordonne-t-elle la publication des lEP?

En janvier 2020, les communications internes ont approché le BDG de la DGOPI concernant les besoins suivants : orientation sur l’établissement de l’ordre de priorité des demandes; archivage continu et plus rapide; mise à jour de la matrice des responsables des lEP; coordination de projets de restructuration à grande échelle; examen, par l’ensemble de la direction générale, des ententes sur les niveaux de service entre les Communications et la DGOPI pour la publication des IEP.

### Ce qui ne change pas?

Les unités de la DGOPI doivent continuer à : tenir à jour leurs IEP; rédiger de nouvelles IEP et mener les consultations adéquates; recevoir des approbations de la direction concernant les lEP; faire la distinction entre les instructions à l’intention des agents et celles à l’intention des clients et collaborer avec leur conseiller en communication stratégique pour créer et mettre à jour leurs pages Web publiques pour les clients.

A4066177_2-000229 mmigaton.R I+1 and Čitzenshp Canesa et Cltoyernihe Cana da

### Outils de transition Pourquoi les unités de la DGOPI doivent-elles désormais sauvegarder une version PDF?

L'UCI a créé : Lorsqu’une page Web est mise à jour, sa version antérieure devient inaccessible. IRCC doit archiver les IEP pour les demandes d’AIPRP et

#### pour les auteurs d'IEP;

On demande aux responsables des IEP de sauvegarder une copie PDF de leurs instructions dès la publication des IEP en ligne. On évitera Des gabarits pour la rédaction ainsi d’écraser une page Web. L'UCl poursuivra la tenue à jour des archives des IEP, qui comprend les tâches suivantes : renommer les fichiers PDF comme il se doit afin de pouvoir les retrouver dans les archives; déplacer les fichiers dans les bons sous-fichiers des archives GCdocs; trouver les fichiers lorsque la version antérieure d’une lEP est demandée.

# Collecte et contrôle des données biométriques et gestion de l’identité

Une gestion de l’identité et des outils d’évaluation des risques efficaces, comme la biométrie, sont essentiels à la lutte contre la fraude, à la gestion de la circulation des personnes et à la protection des intérêts du Canada en matière de sécurité. Le présent document contient les principales directives et procédures pour établir et vérifier l’identité des demandeurs.

## Collecte et contrôle des données biométriques

Les données biométriques, plus particulièrement les empreintes digitales, servent à établir l’identité des demandeurs au moment de la présentation d’une demande et consistent en un outil d’intégrité des programmes. Ce qui suit fournira aux utilisateurs le contexte nécessaire pour comprendre la politique et l’utiliser, et pour comprendre les règles de divulgation associées à la biométrie à CIC. Apprenez-en davantage.

#### Gestion de l’identité

Directives concernant la création de dossiers dans les systèmes de CIC pour tous les secteurs d’activité de CIC, les analyses de l’ADN et d’autres questions liées à l’identité.

##### Gestion de l’identité

### Aperçu

Le présent guide/chapitre peut être utilisé par tous les employés de Citoyenneté et Immigration Canada qui sont chargés de fournir aux clients des services en matière de citoyenneté et d’immigration.

### Objectifs

Les procédures énoncées dans le présent guide fournissent des directives concernant la création d’enregistrements de nom dans les systèmes de CIC pour les secteurs d’activité suivants : citoyenneté, résidence permanente, résidence temporaire et protection des réfugiés. En accord avec laPolitique d’appellation, ces procédures soulignent l’importance d’utiliser la preuve documentaire la plus fiable disponible en ce qui a trait au nom d’une personne, plutôt que d’utiliser le nom indiqué par le client.

Dans la mesure du possible, les procédures sont conformes aux lignes directrices de l’Organisation de l’aviation civile internationale (OACl) énoncées dans le document 9303 de l’OACI sur les titres de voyage lisibles à la machine (TVLM), que l’on peut consulter à l’adresse suivante : www.icao.int. C’est dans le cadre du programme de TVLM de l’OACl que sont établies les normes, les spécifications et les pratiques exemplaires dans les domaines liés aux TVLM, dans le but d’accroître la sécurité nationale et internationale et de faire la promotion des avantages liés à la facilitation.

##### Biométriques (disponible à l’interne seulement)

En accord avec la Politique d’appellation, ces procédures soulignent l’importance d’utiliser la preuve documentaire la plus fiable disponible en ce qui a trait au nom d’une personne, plutôt que d’utiliser le nom indiqué par le client.

Dans la mesure du possible, les procédures sont conformes aux lignes directrices de l’Organisation de l’aviation civile internationale (OACl) énoncées dans le document 9303 de l’OACI sur les titres de voyage lisibles à la machine (TVLM), que l’on peut consulter à l’adresse suivante : www.icao.int. C’est dans le cadre du programme de TVLM de l’OACI que sont établies les normes, les spécifications et les pratiques exemplaires dans les domaines liés aux TVLM, dans le but d’accroître la sécurité nationale et internationale et de faire la promotion des avantages liés à la facilitation.

#### Définition

CIC recueille les données biométriques suivantes : données biographiques; empreintes digitales; photographies. Fait d’enregistrer le nom associé à un client. Confirmation de résidence permanente. Ce document sert de preuve du statut de résident permanent jusqu'à l’obtention de la carte de résident permanent (carte RP). Document servant à faire le lien entre l’ancienne identité de la personne et la nouvelle.

Un document de liaison peut être une décision juridique ou administrative rendue par l’autorité provinciale ou territoriale compétente comme preuve de changement de nom.

La section Changement de nom au Canada : résidents permanents, résidents temporaires et citoyens canadiens contient des exemples de documents de liaison. Direction générale de la gestion opérationnelle et de la coordination

d’autres documents. On peut affirmer que, sur ce document, figure le nom officiel d’une personne lors de la délivrance. Veuillez consulter la liste des documents principaux et l'ordre de priorité à suivre pour créer et enregistrer les noms de client, par secteur d'activité. Direction générale de la gestion opérationnelle et de la coordination Habituellement, le ou les noms de famille sont les identificateurs principaux. Tous les autres éléments d’un nom, habituellement le ou les prénoms, sont les identificateurs secondaires. Loi sur l’immigration et la protection des réfugiés Série de lettres de l’alphabet latin par lequel on identifie un client. Un nom comporte habituellement deux éléments : un identificateur principal et un identificateur secondaire. Organisation de l’aviation civile internationale Document délivré sur la foi d’un document principal. Elle sert à vérifier des éléments relatifs à l’identité, comme la date de naissance ou le nom utilisé par la personne, lequel pourrait différer du nom figurant sur le document principal. Veuillez consulter les types de pièces justificatives délivrées par les provinces et les territoires du Canada. Puce lisible à la machine intégrée à un titre de voyage Pratiques d’appellation en vigueur dans la région ou le pays où le passeport a été délivré Règlement sur l’immigration et la protection des réfugiés Système mondial de gestion des cas Système de soutien aux opérations des bureaux locaux Terme générique faisant référence aux systèmes de CIC, dont le SMGC et le SSOBL Représentation de l’épellation d’un nom ou de lettres d’un alphabet dans les mots ou lettres de l’alphabet latin.

La translittération consiste à changer ou à transcrire l'épellation de mots ou de lettres d'un alphabet dans les mots ou lettres d'un autre alphabet; toutefois, dans le contexte de CIC, la translittération se fait dans l'alphabet latin.

### Gestion de l’identité : Documents utilisés pour établir un nom-Ordre de

priorité

Veuillez consulter l’ordre de priorité des pièces d’identité à utiliser pour établir et enregistrer les noms de client, par secteur d’activité ci-dessous. Soulignons que cette annexe ne s’applique pas aux demandes de changement de nom. Veuillez plutôt consulter la section Demandes de changement de nom pour connaître les pièces justificatives requises à l’appui d’une demande de changement de nom.

La page Documents utilisés pour établir un nom donne plus de détails quant à l’utilisation des pièces d’identité à cette fin.

##### Traductions acceptables

Tout document permettant d’établir le nom qui est rédigé dans une langue autre que le français ou l’anglais (par exemple, un certificat de mariage ou un certificat de naissance) doit être accompagné d’une traduction officielle en français ou en anglais. Le document doit être certifié par une personne autorisée (veuillez consulter la trousse de demande pour obtenir la liste des personnes autorisées), après quoi les copies certifiées doivent être traduites par un traducteur d’un service de traduction qualifié indépendant. Tous les frais afférents à la traduction des documents sont à la charge du client.

Les traductions faites par des personnes non autorisées qui connaissent la langue d’origine, y compris les parents, les amis, les connaissances ou les représentants d’un organisme bénévole, NE SONT PAS ACCEPTÉES.

### Gestion de l’identité : Documents utilisés pour créer des

## enregistrements de nom, par secteur d’activité

#### Résidents temporaires ou résidents permanents

Passeports et autres titres de voyage La grande majorité des clients de CIC qui font une demande de résidence temporaire ou de résidence permanente auront un passeport en leur possession. En tant que document officiel précisant l’identité et la nationalité d’une personne afin de faciliter les déplacements internationaux, le passeport étranger est le document principal à utiliser pour créer un enregistrement de nom pour les besoins de CIC. Veuillez consulter la section Comment lire les titres de voyage pour savoir comment utiliser : la zone de lecture automatique (ZLA); la puce lisible à la machine (PLM); la zone d’inspection visuelle (ZIV) d’un passeport ou d’un titre de voyage pour déterminer le nom qui sera enregistré dans les systèmes de CIC.

Voir la section Structure des noms et comment enregistrer les noms dans le SMGC pour connaître la procédure à suivre pour créer un nom à l’aide d’un passeport.

Si une personne n’a pas de passeport, mais est en possession d’un autre titre de voyage, le nom consigné sur ce titre de voyage servira à créer l’enregistrement de nom pour les besoins de CIC. REMARQUE : sur certains passeports ou titres de voyage, on trouvera une modification officielle dans la section « Observations » du titre de voyage (par exemple, nom marital), auquel cas la modification doit être prise en compte pour établir le nom, et le nom indiqué dans la zone de lecture automatique (ZLA) du passeport doit être enregistré comme ancien nom ou pseudonyme dans le système de CIC. Pour en savoir plus au sujet de la ZLA, veuillez consulter la section Comment lire les titres de voyage pour déterminer le nom qui sera enregistré dans les systèmes de CIC.

Demandeurs d’asile Passeport ou autre titre de voyage délivré par le pays de nationalité Si le demandeur est titulaire d’un passeport ou d’un titre de voyage délivré par son pays de nationalité, veuillez consulter la procédure énoncée dans la section Passeports et autres titres de voyage pour résident temporaire ou résident permanent.

Aucun passeport ni titre de voyage délivrés par le pays de nationalité En l’absence d’un passeport ou d’un titre de voyage délivré par le pays de nationalité, il faut utiliser le nom indiqué sur les autres documents (ou photocopies) fournis par le demandeur, en s’assurant qu’il correspond au nom que la personne a inscrit sur ses formulaires de demande.
